ELECTION COMMISSION OF INDIA 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 3rd February, 1978 

S.O. 486. — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shri Dahyabhai Shamalbhai Valvi, At Sundarpara, 
Taluka Nandod, District Broach (Gujarat), a contesting 
candidate for bye-election to the Gujarat Legislative Assembly 
held in June, 1977 from 160-Rajpipla (ST) constituency, has 
failed to lodge an account of his election expenses as 
required by the Representation of the People Act, 1951, 
and the Rules made thereunder ; 

And whereas, the said candidate, even after due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for the failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Dahyabhai Shamalbhai Valvi to be disqualified for 
being chosen as, and for being, a member of either House 
of Parliament or of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative 
Council of a State for a period of three years from the 
date of this order. 

[No. GJ-LA/160/77(Bye)(l)] 
S. P. RAJE, Under Secy.

New Delhi, the 4th February, 1978 

9.0. 487.— In exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (I) of Section 13A of the Representation of the People 
Act, 1950 (43 of 1950), the Election Commission of India, 
in consultation with the Administration of the Union Terri- 
tory of Chandigarh, hereby nominates Shri R, S, Mann, 
I.A.S., Home Secretary, Chandigarh Administration, as the 
Chief Electoral Officer for the Union Territory of Chandigarh 
with effect from the date he takes charge of the office and 
until further order vice Shri M. G, Devasahayam. 

[No. 154/CH/78] 
V. NAGASUBRAMANIAN, Secy.

MINISTRY OF HOME AFFAIRS 

New Delhi, the. 9th February, 1978 

S.O. 488, — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of section 475 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, 
1973 (2 of 1974), and in supersession of the Criminal 
Courts and Court-martial (Adjustment of Jurisdiction) Rules, 
1952, the Central Government hereby makes the following 
rules for the trial of the persons subject to military, naval 


or air force law, or any other Jaw relating to the Armed 
Forces of the Union by a Court to which the said Code 
applies, or by a Court-martial, namely : — 

1. These rules may be called the Criminal Courts and 
Court-martial (Adjustment of Jurisdiction) Rules, 1978. 

2. In these rules, unless the context otherwise requires. 

(a) "commanding officer”, 

(i) in relation to a person subject to military law, 

means the Officer Commanding the unit to 
which such person belongs or is attached ; 

(ii) in relation to a person subject to naval law, 

means the Commanding Officer of the ship or 

naval establishment to which such person for 
the time being belongs ; and 

(iii) in relation to a person subject to air force 

law, means the officer for the time being in 

command of the unit to which such person 
belongs or is attached ; 

(iv) (b) “competent air force authority" means the 
Chief of the Air Slaff, the air or other officer 
commanding any Command, Group, Wing or 
Station in which the accused person is serving, 
or where such person is serving in a field area, 
the Officer Commanding the forces or the air 
forces in the field ; 

(c) “competent military authority” means the Chief of 

Army Stall or Officer Commanding the army, 
army corps, division, area, sub-area or indepen- 
dent brigade in which the accused person is serv- 
ing, and, except in cases falling under section 69 
of the Army Act, 1950 (46 of 1950) in which 
death has resulted, the officer commanding the 
brigade or sub-area or station in which the accused 
person is serving ; 

(d) "competent naval authority" means the Chief of 

the Naval Stall or the Flag Officer Commanding 
in-Chief, Western Naval Command, Bombay or the 
Flag Officer Commanding-in-Chief, Eastern Naval 
Command Vishakhapatnam or the Flag Officer 
Commanding, Southern Naval Area. Cochin or 
the Flag Officer Commanding, Western Fleet or the 
Flag Officer Commanding, Eastern Fleet or Senior 
Naval Officer where the accused person is serving, 

3. Where a person subject to military, naval or air force 
low, or any other law relating to the Armed Forces of the 
Union for the time being in force is brought before a 
Magistrate and charged with an offence for which he is 
also liable to be tried by a Court-martial, such Magistrate 
shall not proceed to try such person or to commit the case 
to the Court of Session, unless — 

(a) he is moved thereto by a competent military, naval 

or air force authority ; or 

(b) he is of opinion, for reasons to be recorded, that 

he should so proceed or to commit without being 
mowed thereto by such authority. 

4. Before proceeding under clause (b) of rule 3, the 
Magistrate shall give a written notice to the Commanding 
officer or the competent military, naval or air force autho- 
rity, as the case may be, of the accused and until the expiry 
of a period of fifteen days from the date of service of the 
notice he shall not — • 

(a) convict or acquit the accused under section 252, 
sub-sections (1) and (2) of section 255 sub- 
section (1) of section 256 or section 257 of the 
Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973 (2 of 1974), or 
hear him in his defence under section 254 of the 
said Code ; or 

(b) frame in writing a charge against the accused 

under section 240 or sub-section (1) of section 246 
of the said Code ; or 

(c) make an order committing the accused for trial 

to the Court of Session under section 209 of the 
said Code ; or 

(d) make over the case for inquiry' or trial under section 

192 of the said Code, 



526 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 5, 1978/PHALGUNA 6 , 1899 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


5. Where a Magistrate has been moved by the compe- 
tent military, naval or air force authority, as the case may 
be, under clause (a) of rule 3, and the commanding officer 
of the accused or the competent military, naval or air force 
authority, as the case may be, subsequently gives notice 
to such Magistrate that, in the opinion of such officer or 
authority, the accused should be tried by a Court-martial, 
such Magistrate if he has not taken any action or made 
any order referred to in clauses (a), (b), (c) or (d) of 
rule 4, before receiving the notice shall stay the proceed- 
ings and, if the accused is in his power or under his con- 
trol, shall deliver him together with the statement referred 
to in sub-section (1) of section 475 of the said Code to 
the officer specified in the said sub-section. 

6. Where within the period of fifteen days mentioned 
in rule 4, or at any time thereafter but before the Magis- 
trate takes any action or makes any order referred to in 
that rule, the commanding officer of the accused or the 
competent military, naval or air force authority, as the 
case may be, gives notice to the Magistrate that in the 
opinion of such officer or authority, the accused should be 
tried by a Court-martial, the Magistrate shall stay the pro- 
ceedings, and if the accused is in his power or under his 
control, shall deliver him together with the statement refer- 
red to in sub-section (1) of section 475 of the said Code 
to the officer specified in the said sub-section. 

7. (1) When an accused has been delivered by the 
Magistrate under rule 5 or 6, the commanding officer of the 
accused or the competent military, naval or air force 
authority, as the case may be, shall, as soon as may be, 
inform the Magistrate whether the accused has been tried 
by a Court-martial or other effectual proceedings have been 
taken or ordered to be taken against him. 

(2) When the Magistrate has been informed under sub- 
rule (1) that the accused has not been tried or other 
effectual proceedings have not been taken or ordered to be 
taken against him. the Magistrate shall report the circum- 
stances to the State Government which may, in consultation 
with the Central Government, take appropriate steps to 
ensure that the accused person is dealt with in accordance 
with law. 

8. Notwithstanding anything in the foregoing rules, where 
It conies to the notice of a Magistrate that a person subject 
to military, naval or air force law, or any other law relat- 
ing to the Armed Forces of the Union for the time being 
In force has committed an offence, proceedings in respect 
of which ought to be instituted before him and that the 
presence of such person cannot be procured except through 
military, naval or air force authorities, the Magistrate may 
by a written notice require the commanding officer of such 
person either to deliver such person to a Magistrate to be 
named in the said notice for being preceded against accord- 
ing to law, or to stay the proceedings against such person 
before the Court-martial if since instituted, and to make 
a reference to the Central Government for determination 
as to the Court before which proceedings should be institu- 
ted. 

9. Where a person subject to military, naval or air force 
law, or any other law relating to the Armed Forces of the 
Union for the time being in force has committed an offence 
which in the opinion of competent military naval or air 
force authority, as the case may be, ought to be tried by 
a Magistrate ip accordance with the civil law in force or 
where the Central Government has, on a reference men- 
tioned in rule 8, decided that proceedings against such 
person should be instituted before a Magistrate, the com- 
manding officer of such person shall after giving a written 
notice to the Magistrate concerned, deliver such person 
under proper escort to that Magistrate. 

[F. No, 23/23 /75-Judl.] 
P. K. DATTA, Dy. Secy.

(Department of Personnel & Administrative Reforms) 
New Delhi, the 18th February, 1978 

. .S.O. 489 — in exercise of Ihc powers conferred by sub- 
section (6) of section 24 of the Code of Criminal Proce- 
dure, 1973, (2 of 1974), the Central Government hereby 
appoints Shri Rani Jethmaiani, Advocate, as a Special 
Public Prosecutor to appear and conduct prosecution Case 
in trial, revision and all other matters relating to the court 
cases arising out of Delhi Special Police Establishment

Where an Income-tax Circle, Ward and District or part 
thereof stands transferred by this notification from one range 
to another range, appeals arising out of assessments made in 
that Income-tax Circle Ward or District or part thereof and 
pending immediately before the date of this notification before 
the Appellate Assistant Commissioner of Income-tax from

section (1) of section 122 of the Income-Tax Act, 1961 
(43 of 1961) and of all other powers enabling it in that

behalf and in partial modification of Notification No, 1703 
(F. No. 261/17/77-ITJ dated 1-4-1977 the Central Board 
of Direct Taxes hereby direct that the following entries 
appearing in col. 3 against Serial No. 1, Range-I, Meerut 
shall be deleted and the remaining entries in Col. 3 will be 
renumbered as 12, 13, 14 and 15. 

“12. Circlc-I, Special Ward, Meerut". 

Where an Income (ax Circlc/Ward or District or part 
thereof stands transferred by this Notification from one 
Range to another Range, appeals arising out of assessments 
made in the Income tax Circle, Ward, or District or part 
thereof and pending immediately before the date of this 
Notification before the A.A.C. of Income tax Range from 
whom that Income tax Circle, ward or District or part 
thereof is transferred shall from the date of this notification 
takes effect be transferred to and dealt with by the A.A.C. 
of Income tax of the Range to wdiom the said Circle ward 
or District or part thereof is transferred. 

This Notification shall take effect from 10-10-1977.

S.O. 506. — In extreiae of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of section 122 of the Income Tax Act, 1961 
(43 of 1961) and of all other powers enabling it in that 
behalf and in partial modification of all previous notifica- 
tions in this regard the Central Board of Direct Taxes 
hereby directs that the Appellate Assistant Commissioner 
of Income-tax, G-Range shall and A.A.C. ‘H’ Range shall 
not perform their functions in respect of appeals filed on 
or after 1-10-1977 against the orders of the Income tax 
officers Distt. VI(4), VT(5), VI(9) & VI (10) in respect of the 
persons and incomes assessed to Income tax or Sur tax etc. 
by them. .jtf 

This notification shall take effect from 10-10-1977.

INCOME-TAX 

S.O. 513. — In exercise of the powers, conferred by 
sub-section (1) of Section 122 of the Tncome-tax Act, 1961 (43 
of 1961) and of all other powers enabling it in this behalf and 
in supersession of all the previous notifications in this regard, 
the Central Board of Direct Taxes hereby directs that the Appel- 
late Asstt. Commissioners of Income-tax of the Ranges specified 
in column 1 of the Schedule below, shall perform their functions 
in respect of all persons and incomes assessed to income-tax 
or super-tax in the income-tax circles, wards and districts speci- 
fied in the corresponding entry in column 2 thereof: — 

SCHEDULE

and XXV, Calcutta.

XXVI, XXIX, Calcutta. 

Central Range-Ill, Calcutta. 

Central Circle : XXVII, 
XXVIII, XXX, XXXI, XXXII 
and XXXIIT, Calcutta. 

Where an income-tax Circle, Ward or District or part thereof 
stands transferred by this notification from one Range to another 
Range appeals arising out of assessments made in that in- 
come-tax Circle, Ward or District or part thereof and pending 
immediately before the date of this notification before the Appel- 
late Asstt, Commissioner of Income-tax of the Range from whom 
that Income-tax Circle, Ward or District or part thereof is trans- 
298 G 1/77—3 


ferred shall from the date this notification take effect, be trans- 
ferred to and dealt with by the appellate Assistant Commissioner 
of the Range to whom the said Circle, Ward or District or part 
thereof is transferred. 

This Notification shall take effect from 14-11-77. 

Explanatory Note : 

The amendment has become necessary consequent on redistri- 
bution of work amongst the A.A.Cs. under Calcutta (Central) 
Charge. 

(This does not form part of the notification but is intended 
to be merely clarificatory).

CENTRAL EXCISE 

S.O. 515. — In exercise of the powers conferred on me by 
rule 5 of the Centra) Excise Rules, 1944, 1 hereby authorise 
the undermentioned Central Excise Officers to exercise the 
powers of the Collector to the extent as shown against each 
in respect of condonation of delay under Rule 173RK(2) 
where the assessee fails to discharge the duty liability within 
the time limit specified under Rule 173RD(2) : 

(a) Superintendent — For condoning delay up to 6 days. 

(b) Assistant Collector — For condoning delay further 

upto one month.

(Department of Foreign Trade 
Office of the Dy. CWef Controller of Imports and Exports, 
Ahmedabad 
ORDER 

Ahmedabad, the 26th September, 1977 

S.O. 518. — M/s. Popular Process Studio, Near Novelty 
Cinema Gheekanta Road. Ahmedabad has been granted 
import licence No. P/S/1858780 dt. 16-6-1976 for 
Rs. 10,000 (Rs, Ten thousand only} for item Highly Poli- 
shed copper sheets etc. as per AM77 policy Book. 

They have applied for duplicate exchange control copy 
of said licence for Rs. 10,000 on the ground that the origi- 
nal exchange control copy has been lost/misplaced without 
having been registered with any custom authority and 
utilised at all. 

In support of their claim applicant has filed an affidavit. 

I am satisfied that exchange control copy of licence 
No. P/S/1858780 dt. 16-6-1976 has been lost/misplaced 
and direct that duplicate copy of exchange control copy of 
the licence should be issued to the applicant. 

The original exchange control copy of licence No, P/S/ 
1858780 dt. 16-6-1976 is cancelled. 

[F. No. 808/EU/17932/NS/AM76i/SSriAU] 

N. C. DAVE, Dy. Chief Controller

(Ofllce of (he Chief Controller of Imporls and Exports, 
New Delhi) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 9th January, 1978 

S.O. 519. — M/s. Minerals & Metals Trading Corporation 
of India Ltd., New Delhi were granted Import Licence 
No. G/I/24324/10/C/XX/64/H/77 dated 23-8-1977 under 
G.C.A. for Rs. 1,00,00,000 (Rupees One Ciorc only) for 
the import of rough Diamonds (GEM QUALJTV). 

2. The firm have requested for issue of duplicate Customs 
Purpose copy of the above mentioned licence on the ground 
that the original Customs Purpose Copy has been misplaced 
by them. It has been further reported by the licensee that 
the customs purpose copy has been misplaced after having 
been registered with Bombay Customs House and utilised 
partly leaving an un-utilised balance of Rs. 65,06,711. 

3. In support of their contention, the applicant have 
filed an affidavit duly sworn in before a Notary in Maha- 
rashtra State. The undersigned is satisfied that the original 
Customs Purpose Copy of Import Licence No. G/T/2432410 
dated 23-8-1977 has been lost and directs that a Duplicate 
Customs Purpose copy of the said licence should be issued 
to the applicant. The original Customs purpose copy of the 
licence is cancelled. 

4. The duplicate customs purpose copy of the licence is 
being issued separately. 

[No. MMTC/4 1-45/77-78 /RM. CELL/RM, 1/589]

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 6th February, 1978 

S.O. 520. — M/s. Bharat Pulverising Mills Pvt. Ltd. Bombay 
were gathered import licence No. P/D/2199840 dated 
28-6-1975 for import of raw materials for manufacture of 
Malathion for Rs. 10,43,000 under IDA credit, They 
have requested for issue of the duplicate Customs Pur- 
poses Copy of the licence on the grounds that the original 
Custom Purposes Copy of the licence has been lost. It 



544 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 25, I978/PHALGUNA 6, 1899 [Part II— Sec, 3(ii)] 


has further been stated that the Customs Purposes Copy of 
the licence in question was lost after utilising Rs. 1,26,030 
and that the same had been registered with the Collector of 
Customs, Bombay. 

2. In support of their contention, the applicant has 
filed an affidavit. The undersigned is satisfied that the 
Original Customs Purposes Copy of Import Licence No. 
P/D/21 99840 dated 28-6-1975 has been lost and directs 
that duplicate Customs Purposes Copy of the said Licence 
should be issued to them. The Original Customs Purposes 
Copy of the Licence is cancelled, 

[No. Ch/B-35(14)/A.M. 75/R. M. Ill] 
G. S. GREWAL, Dy, Chief Controller

MINISTRY OF CIVIL SUPPLIES & COOPERATION 

New Delhi, ihe 9th February, 1978 

S.O. 522.— The Central Government, in consultation with 
the Forward Markets Commission, having considered the 
application for renewal of recognition made under Section 5 
of the Forward Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1952 (74 of 
1952) by Ihe Kaiscrganj Beopar Company Ltd., Meerut and 
being satisfied that it would be in the interest of the trade 
and also in the public interest so lo do, hereby grants in 
exercise of the powers conferred by Section 6 of the said 
Act recognition to the said Company for a further period 
of two years from the 10th August, 1976 lo 9th August, 
1978 (both days inclusive) in respect of forward contracts 
in gur. 

2. The recognition deemed to have been granted is 'Subject 
to the condition that the said Company shall comply with 
such directions as may, from time to tittle, be given by the 
Forward Markets Commission. 

[F. No. I2(24)-IT/77] 
V. SRINIVASAN, Dy, Secy.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM 

New Delhi, the 30th January, 1978 

S.O. 526. — Whereas by a notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Petroleum, S. O. No. 1516 dated 
21-4-1977 under sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petro- 
leum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of right of user 
in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central Government 
declared its intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands specified in the schedule appended to that notification 
for the purpose of laying pipeline ; 

And whereas the competent Authority has under sub- 
section (1) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to 
the Government ; 

And further whereas the Central Government has after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the right 
of user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to 
this notification ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of section 6 of the said Act, the Cen- 
tral Government hereby declares that the right of user in 
the said lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification hereby acquired for laying the pipelines ; 


And further in exercise of power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of that section, the Central Government directs that the 
right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in 
the Central Government vest on this date of the publication 
of this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission 
free from encumbrances.

MINISTRY OF WORKS & HOUSING 

New Delhi, the 15th February, 1978 

S.O. 535. — Whereas certain modification, which the 
Central Government proposes to make in the Master Plan 
for Delhi regarding the areas mentioned hereunder, was pub- 
lished with Notice No. F. 20(8) /77-MP, dated the 1st Octo- 
ber, 1977, in accordance with the provisions of section 44 
of the Delhi Development Act, 1957 (61 of 1957) inviting 
objections/suggestions, as required by sub-section (3) of 
section 1 1-A of the said Act, within thirty days from the 
date of the said notice ; 

And whereas no objections or suggestions have been re- 
ceived witli regard to the aforesaid modification, the Central 
Government have decided to modify the Master Pi'an for 
Delhi ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2) of section 11-A of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby makes the following modification in the 
said Master Plan tor Delhi with effect from the dale of 
publication of this notification in the Gazette of India, 
namely 

MODIFICATION ; 

‘The land use of an area measuring 3.24 heels. (8.00 
acres) forming part of the land earmarked for 
“recreational” land-use in the Master Plan located in 
the cast of District Centre (Nehru Place) and in the 
north of Kalkaji Temple falling in Zone F-2 (Kai j 
lash) is changed to “public and semi-public facilities 
(religious)”.’

New Delhi, the 27th January, 1978 

S.O. 540 — In exercise of the powers conferred by 
Section 5 of the (Administration of Evacuee Property Act. 
1950 (XXXI of 1950), the Central Government hereby 
appoints Shri B. D. Dhalia, Deputy Secretary (Rehabilita- 
tion) in the Rehabilitation Department of the State Govern- 
ment of Haryana as Assistant Custodian General of Evacuee 
Property for the purpose of discharging the duties imposed 
on such Assistant Custodian General by or under the said 
Act with immediate elfect. This supersedes the notification 
No. 1 (8) /Spl. Cell/77-SS. II dated the 10th August, 1977.

New Delhi, the 31st January, 1978 

S.O. 541.-— In exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub section (1) of Section 3 of the Displaced Persons (Com- 
pensation and Rehabilitation) Act 1954 (44 of 1954), the 
Central Government hereby appoints all Collectors in State 
of Rajasthan, to be Settlement Commissioners for the purpose 
of performing, in addition to their own duties as Collectors, 
within their jurisdiction the functions assigned to a Settle- 
ment Commissioner by or under the said Act in relation 
to the work entrusted to the Government of Rajasthan hv 
the Government of India in the Department of Rehabilita- 
tion under the. “Package Deal” or “Administrative and 
Financial arrangements”.

New Delhi, the 27th January, 1978 

S.O. 542.— In exercise of the powers conferred on 
me as Custodian General by sub-section (3) of Section 55 of 
the Administration of Evacuee Property Act, 1950 (31 of 
1950), I do hereby delegate to the Assistant Custodian 
General for the State of Haryana, appointed vide this 
Department’s notification No. 1 (8) /Spl. Cell/77-SS. II dated 
the 27th January, 1978, the following powers of the Custo- 
dian General : — 

(i) Powers under Section 24 and 27 of the Act 

(ii) Powers of approval of transfer of any evacuee 
property under Section 10(2) (0) of the Act. 

(iii) Power of transfer of cases under Rule 30-A of the 
Administration of Evacuee Property (Central) 
Rules, 1955. 

This supersedes notification No. 1 (8)),/Spl. CcIl/77-SS. II 
dated 10th August, 1977. 

[No. 1(8) /Spl. Cell/77-SS. II] 
A. C. BANDYOPADHYAY, Custodian General.

MINISTRY OF LABOUR 

New Delhi, the 2nd December, 1977 
ORDER 

S.O. 453.— Whereas the Central Government is of 
opinion that an industrial dispute exists between the employers 
in relation to the management of stevedores of Cochin Port 
specified in the Schedule 1 annexed hereto and their work- 
men in respect of the matters specified in the Schedule 11 
hereto annexed ; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desir- 
able to refer the said dispute for adjudication ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Section 7 A and clause (d) of sub-section (1) of Section 10 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the 
Central Government hereby constitutes an industrial Tribunal 
of which Shri K. Selvaratnam shall bo the Presiding Officer 
with headquarters at Madras and refers the said dispute for 
adjudication to the said Tribunal. 

SCHEDULE I 

1. Messrs Paul Abrao & Sons, Stevedores, Cochin- 

682003. 

2. Messrs South India Corporation (Private) Limited, 

Stevedores, Cochin-682003,

Cochin-682003.

682002. 

SCHEDULE II 

Whether the stevedores of Cochin Port are justified In 
not granting to the photo pass casual workers the 
arrears from 1-1-1974 to 31-3-1975 in terms of new 
wage rates recommended by the Wage Revision 
Committee as accepted by the Government of India 
in Resolution No. LWR-20/77-D. II dated the 17th 
August, 1977 of the Ministry of Shipping and 
Transport ? If not, to what relief are these work- 
men entitled 7

S.O. 544.— In exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of Section 5 of the Mines Act, 1952 (35 of 
1952) and in supersession of the notificaiton of the Govern- 
ment of India in the Ministry of Labour S.O. No. 4285 
dated the 12th October, 1976, the Central Government hereby 
appoints Shri B. M. Bhat, to be the Chief Inspector of Mines 
with effect from the 1st February, 1978 for all the territories 
to which the said Act extends, until further orders.

New Dolhi, the 6th February, 1978 
ORDER 

S O. 545.— Whereas the industrial disputes specified in the Schedule hereto annexted, are pending before Shri M.U. Shah, Presid- 
ing Officer, Industrial Tribunal Ahmedabad : 

And, whereas, the services of Shri M.U. Shah are no longer available; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 7A and sub-section (1) of section 33-B of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947(14 of 1947), the Central Gvcrnment hereby constitutean Industrial Tribunal ofwhichShri R.C. Israni, B. A. (Hons)L.L.B. shall be the 
Presiding Officer with Headquarters at Ahmedabad and Withdraws the proceedings in relation to the said disputes from the aforesaid former 
Tribunal and transfer the same to the latter Industrial Tribunal, Ahmedabad presided by Shri R. C. Israni and directs that the said Tribunal 
shall proceed with the same proceeding from the stage at which they are transferred to it and dispose off the same according to the law.

New Delhi, the 7th February, 1978 

S.O. 546.— Whereas the Stato Government of Punjab 
has, in pursuance of clause (d) of section 4 of the Emp- 
loyees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), nominated 
Shri O. Balakrlshnan, Secretary to Government of Punjab, 
Health and Family Welfare Department, Chandigarh to 
represent that State on the Employees’ State Insurance Cor- 
poration, in place of Smt. Usha Vohrn; 

Now. therefore, in pursuance of section 4 of the Emp- 
loyees’ State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central 
Government hereby makes the following amendment in the 
notification Of the Government of Tndia in the Ministry of 
Labour No. S.O. 1517, dated the 14th April, 1976, 
namely 

In the said notification, under the heading “(Nominated 
by the State Governments under clause (d) of section 4)’’, 
for the entry against. Item 22, (he following entry shall be 
substituted, namely : — 

“Shri G, Balakrishnan, 

Secretary to the Government of Punjab, 

Health and Family Welfare Department, 

Chandigarh.’’

S.O. 548. — Whereas it appears to the Central Govern- 
ment (hat the employer and the majority of the employees 
in relation to the establishment known as Messrs Archana 
Food Products (Private) Limited, 18/23, J.K. Industrial 
Estate, Mahakali Caves Road, Andhcri (East), Bombay-93 
including its Office at 901, Prasad Chambers, Opera House, 
Bombay-4, have agreed that the provisions of the Employees’ 
Provident Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act. 1952 (19 
of 1952), should be made applicable to the said establish- 
ment ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of tho powers conferred by 
sub-section (4) of section 1 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby applies the provisions of the said Act 
to the said establishment. 

This notification shall be deemed to have come into force 
on the first day of January, 1977.

[No. U-16012(17)/76-HI] 

S. S. S All A SRAN A MAN, Dy. Secy. 


justified ? If not to what relief is the concerned 
workman entitled ?” 

2. It is not disputed that Hukam Sai was charge-sheeted 
alongwith the other four workmen for habitual absence which 
is a misconduct under Sub-Clause (d) of Standing Order 
No. 17(i). On his alleged admission to the said charge the 
case was held to be proved against him and was consequently 
dismissed from service, while others escaped unscathed be- 
cause it is said they had no such previous history. 

3. Union’s case is that Hukam Sai had worked as a domes- 
tic servant at the residence of Sri U. Kumar, Deputy Chief 
Mining Engineer, where once he happened to damage the 
Saree of the wife of Sri U. Kumar. On this the value of 
that Saree was recovered from his pay. The Union at the 
instance of Hukam Sal, agitated against the malpractice of 
engaging the workmen of the colliery as domestic servants 
and vide a directive from the Head Office that practice was 
discontinued. The Management became annoyed with Hukam 
Sai on that account and it was due to that personal spite 
that his absence was manipulated by not giving him work 
during the said months of January to April 1975 and he was 
charge-sheeted for remaining absent, though in fact he always 
attended the colliery but was even then not allotted any work. 
His thumb mark was obtained on a paper on the plea 
that it was necessary to sign suclt a paper before he can be 
taken back oil duly. That paper was then converted into 
a reply of admission to the charge. In fact he had never 
admitted the charge. On the basis of that admission he 
was dismissed which punishment was disproportionately severe 
and discriminatory. The action was malande being revenge- 
ful. Alleged previous history of irregular attendance has been 
denied. 


New Delhi, the 4th February, 1978 

S-0. 571. — In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Govern- 
ment Industrial Tribunal, Jabalpur in the industrial dis- 
pute between the employers in relation to the management of 
Churcha Colliery of Western Coalfields l imited. District 
Surguja (Madhya Pradesh) and their workmen, which was 
received by the Central Government on 2nd February, 1978. 

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL- 

CUM-LABOU'R COURT, JABALPUR. 

Case No. C.GIT /LC(R)(10) of 1976 

PARTIES : , 

Employers in relation to the Management of Churcha 
Colliery of Western Coalfields Limited, Post Office 
Churcha, District Surguja (M.P.) and their work- 
men represented through the M.P, Koyla Mazdoor 
Panchayat (HMS) P.O. Kurasia. District Surguja 
(M.P-). 

APPEARANCES : 

For Management ... Sri J. D. Sinnukadam, 

Senior Law Officer 

For Workmen ... Sri Hardeo Singh, 

President. 

INDUSTRY : Coal Mines DISTRICT : Surguja (M.P.) 
Dated : January, 23, 1978 

AWARD 

This is a reference made by the Government of India in 
the Ministry of Labour vide its order No, L. 22012/33/ 
7Ji/DIII(B), dated the 29th Match, 1976, of the following 
industrial dispute for adjudication by this Tribunal : 

“Whether the action of the Management of Churcha 
Colliery of Western Coalfields Limited, Post Office 
Churcha, District Surguja (Madhya Pradesh) in 
dismissing Sri Hukum Sai, Category I Mazdoor of 
Churcha Colliery with effect from 29-9-1975 js 


4. The Management denied the alleged incident of 
damaging the Saree or recovery of its value or discontinuance 
of the system of keeping domestic servants at the instance of 
Hukam Sai or the Union. The reply admitting the charges 
was voluntary and genuine and the history of irregular atten- 
dance put his case on a different footing from others who 
had no such history. 

5. Preliminary objections against Union's representative 
capacity and invalidity of the reference on account of the 
dispute being not sponsored by a representative Union were 
rejected vide order dated 17-9-1977. On the other hand the 
other pica of Hukam Sai being defiauded to sign the reply 
containing admission to the charge Was allowed. Consequently 
an opportunity was given to the Management to establish the 
charge before this Tribunal. Accordingly both parties ad- 
duced evidence before the commission and -artly before me. 
A duty has now befallen upon (his Tribunal to decide 
whether the said charge of misconduct punishable under 
Clause 17 (i) (d ) of the Standing Orders is established hv 
evidence or not. 

6. Before deciding the preliminary issues Commission was 
issued for recording the evidence. Management examined 4 
witnesses before the Commissioner and the remaining two 
who could not be so examined were then examined before me. 
Thereafter when the case proceeded on merits Commission 
was again issued. Management recalled some of the witnes- 
ses and examined them on merits besides examining a set 
of new witnesses, All these statements have been consolidated 
and marked by me as MW-1 to MW-1L In reply the 
Union examined three witnesses including Hukam Sal before 
the Commissioner and three other witnesses before the 
second Commissioner. They are serially marked as WW-1 
to WW-6. 


7. Sn M. M. Yohnnan (WW-1) says that Hukam Sai 
started complaining to him from February 1975 that he 
was not being allowed to work at the residence of Sri 
U-. Kumar, Deputy C.M.E, On the other hand Hukam 
Sai (WW-2) has himself stated that 9 months before his 
stoppage from, work in May 1975 i.e. some time since 
August 1974 he was shifted to the mine. Since then there 
was no question of his complaining that he was not being 
allowed to work at the residence of Sri U. Kumar The 
testimony of Sri Yohnnan (WW-1) is thus patently false 

8. Again Sri M. M. Yohnnan (WW-1) stated that Hukum 
Sai was stopped from work on the ground of the damage 
which he had caused to the Saree of Smt. Kumar when 

e- W ™mU°^' n F* at J h !? re , sidence ' ° n tf »e other hand Hukam 
Sai (WW-2) stated that he was stopped from work not be- 



[wt Il~^3(ii)] *rror irr : 


cause of the Saree incident but because he had refuse to spare 
the labourers lor th<" work cf Vinod Sirdar, According to him 
he was in-charge of some labourers, These are thus the 
only two statements made by Sri Yohnnan (WW-1). On 
both these points, he has been contradicted to Hi kam Sai. 
His statement is therefore wholly unworthy of credence. So 
lar its Hukum Sal’s statement is concerned, it was for the first 
time that he ihtroduce the story of his refusal to supply 
labourers to Vinod Sirdar when he appeared in the witness 
box. This story never appeared in the pleadings nor any 
such question was ever asked in cross-examination to any of 
the witnesses of the Management, According to his state- 
ment as WW-2 It was under the direction of Sri Bahadur 
that he refused to spare the labourers and that was the 
main cause of his trouble. As against that Sri N. N, 
Bahadur (MW-2) has stated that llukam Sai was Category 1 
Mazdoor. No labourer was put under hitn because he him- 
self belonged to tile lowest category and he never gave him 
any instructions not to spare any labourer to any other person 
because there was no point to issue any such instructions 
to him. The statement of Sri Bahadur appears to be correct 
in as much as a Category I Mazdoor is not likely to be 
placed inchnrge of other labourers because he is himself 
lowest in the rank. Moreover, the story as narrated by 
Hukam Sal was never pleaded as stated before. The state- 
ment of Hukam Shi on this point appears to be totally false. 

9. Management’s evidence proves convincingly that Hukam 
Sai remained absent in the months of January to April 
1975. There is no reason to disbelieve that evidence. There 
is nothing to show' that his absence was manipulated by not 
marking him present and not giving hint work even when he 
was present Witness after witness has denied any instruc- 
tion of the superior officers on this point. Time keepers 
have stated that they did mark him present whenever he 
appeared before them and at least after his posting to the 
mine it was incumbent on him to gef his attendance mark- 
ed before he could be allotted the work. Merc assertion by 
Hukam Sai that he continued to remain present throughout 
this period can hardly be believed in the light of the over- 
whelming evidence of the Management on this point. 

10. Hud it been true that Hukam Sai was so kept out of 
duty and without payment of wages, ho would not have 
spared the Management so easily. He must have raised hue 
and cry directly or through the Union specially when, a suffi- 
ciently long period passed without payment of 
wages. It Is not clear why a complaint was not made to the 
superior officers that Hukam Sai was kept out of work and 
without payment of wages. Solitary vague statement of 
Bhini Naik (WW-6) (Sri M, M. Yohnnan, WW-1 has 
already been disbelieved) that Hukam Sai used to meet 
him once or twice a week and used to complain that he was 
not being given work in the colliery, carries no 
weight. It is not clear to which period his statement relates. 
He does not say how and why an underground workman, 
who was stopped from work, had occasion to meet a 
person working in a Rest House who worked only part time 
on Conveyor Belt. 

11. The general argument as to why should a work- 

man remain absent knowing that he will thereby 
lose wages, has no relevance. Even the Wage Board 

Report indicates the anxiety of the employers about the 
chronic absenteeism prevailing in the mines. There can lie 
several causes or excuses for remaining absent. The har- 
vesting season, availability of some other temporary work 
on better remuneration, need of some more urgent work at 
home town, illness of self or other member of (he family 
and so on, the list can be multiplied according to the 

varied needs of man and as per exigencies of the situation. 

12. It is argued that (he attendance register could be 

tampered with and the vertical line put for marking present 
could any time be changed to Capital ‘A’. What could be 
done is not necessarily what was actually done. Here we 
are not concerned with possibilities or probabilities bul with 
the hard facts emerging out of the clear evidence. In this 
wav [ am inclined to hold that, there Is clear unrebutted 

evidence that Hukam Sui remained absent from January to 
April 1975, 

13. Though the charge framed by the disciplinary autho- 
rity contented Itself merely by making a vague mention of 
habitual absence without giving the specific dates of absence 
on previous occasions yet the vagueness did not persist 


qTCTtf 25, 1 0 7 e/HtfiCT 0, 18 99 599 


when proceedings started in this Tribunal inasmuch as the 
pleadings -specifically mentioned that Hukam Sai remained 
absent without permission ftom 21td April 1972 to 11th 
September 1972 for which punishment, of stoppage of incre- 
ments for three years was awarded and thereafter he again 
remained absent from 3/4/1974 to 18/5/1974 for which 
a warning was given to him. A rejoinder to these allegations 
made in the preliminary objection, filed by the Management 
on 6/8/1975, was filed by the Union on 24th August 1976, 
but not a word of denial of these factual allegations appeared 
in it. Thereby it will he deemed that the Union admitted 
these facts and thus on the basis of implied admission in 
pleadings it can be held that Hukam Sai did remain absent 
on the aforesaid two occasions in the years 1972 and 1974. 

14. liven otherwise also the cross-examination indicates 
that an attempt was made by the Union leader to establish 
from the witnesses of the Management that the attendance 
of the domestic servant, as Hukam Sai was, during the years 
1972 to 1974 was marked after ascertaining the fact from 
the officer concerned or his wife if the employee himself 
did not turn up in the morning or evening to gel his atten- 
dance marked in the lime office. From this Sri Hnrdeo 
Singh wants to draw an inference that if the officer concerned 
happened to be displeased with the domestic servant for 
some reason or the other he would not confirm that the 
domestic servant had attended at the residence and that 
would result in the workman being marked absent. This 
however is only a hypothetical and presumptive possibility 
and there is no evidence that this possibility ever materialised 
into a factual situation. 

15. In any case this could be possible for the absence 

being marked for a day or two or at the most for a week 
because the absence would result in deduction of wages and 
naturally the domestic servant may at the most tolerate such 
deductions for a day or two or for a week but it is not 
possible that deductions for months together may be swallow- 
ed by him without raising a voice. How is the domestic 
servant going to maintain himself if he continues to work 
and does not get his pay for months together ? The absence 
from 21/4/1972 to 11/9/1972 is absence of about 4J 
months. Similarly the absence from 3/4/1974 to 18/5/1974 
is absence of about If months. Can it be imagined that 
Hukam Sai remained undisturbed and unagitated even 

when he did not get wages for such long periods in spite 
of his continued working with Sri Kumar ? 't he length of 
the period by itself negatives the conjecture which is sought 
to be raised by Sri Hardeo Singh. The charge-sheets for 

the absence during these periods have been proved nlongwith 
the final orders that were passed. The fact that the final 
orders were not communicated to the workman or the 
warning letter Ex. E/7 was not served upon him has no 
relevance in the present case. That service or no service has 
no bearing. !n fact copy of the warning letter Ex. E/7 
purports to have been sent to Hukam Sai and simply non 
production of the despatch receipt register etc. cannot lead 
to the conclusion that the letter was not' served upon him. 
Hukam Sai’s mere denial is not a sufficient rebuttal of this 
evidence. It is thus held that absence of Hukam Sai without 
permission for long periods on. more than one occasions 
in the past is established by cogent evidence. . Under the 
circumstances I am inclined to hold that the charge of 
Clause (d) of Standing Order No. 17 (i) i s fully established 
against Hukam Sai. In view of this finding the fact that 
the Management was having a spite against him is of little 

consequence, as Management’s spite never asked him to 

remain absent from duty for long periods. 

16. This brings us to the question of quantum of punish- 
ment which is alleged to be too severe and vindictive and 
discriminatory. The other four workmen who were facing 
a similar charge were not punished because they had no suen 
history of habitual absence. There is no reason to disbelieve 
this explanation of the Management. Tt cannot be disbelieved 
simply because Hardeo Singh suddenly wanted a witness to 
produce the service sheets etc. of those workmen within nn 
hour and the witness expressed his inability to do so_ at 
such a short notice. Tf Sri Hardeo Singh wanted to rely on 
them proper notice for production should have been given. 
Distinction between a workmen who remains absent habi- 
tually and those who arc not addicted to that bad habit docs 
not suffer from the vice of discrimination. 

17. A habitual absentee who does not heed to previous 
warnings and instead of giving a satisfactory explanation for 
his absence raises an issue tinder tho cover of some Union 
seeks a licence to continue to commit misconduct with im- 
punity and therefore deserves no sympathy at the hands of 



600 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 5, 1978/PHALGUNA 6 , 1899 [Par il— Sec. 3(ii)] 


the Tribunal which cannot be instrumental to the perpetration 
ol chaotic indiscipline in an industry. Seriousness of proved 
misconduct cannot be liquidated by an allegation of some 
previous spite or vindictiveness. 

18. I am thus not inclined to interfere with the quantum 
of punishment (dismissal) awarded by the Management. 
Facts and circumstances do call for such a punishment. It 
is, therefore, held that the Management was justified in dis- 
missing Hukam Sai. Reference is answered accordingly. 

S. N. JOHRI, Presiding Officer

(In the matter of the dispute for adjudication under 
Section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947 between the workmen and Shri R. Venkata- 
chalam, Raising Contractor of Ponkumar Magnesite 
Mines, Salem.) 

BETWEEN 

The workmen represented by The Acting General Sec- 
retary, Salem District Magnesite Labour Union, 237,

AND

REFERENCE : 

Order No. L-29012/1 1 /77-D.UIB, dated 16-4-1977 of the 
Ministry of Labour, Government of India, 

This dispute coming on this day for final hearing upon 
perusing the reference, claim and counter statements and all 
other material papers on record and upon hearing of Thim 
N. G. R. Prasad, Partner of Thlruvalargal Row and Reddy 
and K. Chandru, advocates for the workmen and of Thiru 
R. Arumugam for Thiruvalargal Aiyar and Dolia, advocates 
for the Management and the parties filed a memorandum of 
settlement settling the dispute and recording the same, this 
Tribunal made the following 

AWARD 

This is an Industrial Dispute for adjudication by the Gov- 
ernment of India under Section 1 0( 1 )(d) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 between the Management of Shri R. Vcnka- 
tachalam, Raising Contractor of Ponkumar Magnesite Mines, 
Salem and their workmen in the matter of dismissal of a 
worker Shri Krishnan. 

(2) The following is the reference. 

Whether the action of Shri R. Venkatachalam, Raising 
Contractor of Ponkumar Magnesite Mines, Salem is 
justified in dismissing Shri Krishnan with effect from 
6-12-1976 7 If not, what relief is he entitled to ? 


(3) The workmen filed a Claim Statement which contained 
the following averments : Krishnan was employed under the 
Respodent Contractor as a worker in the mines for the last 
2 years and he had a good record of service and was a 
member of the Petitioner- Union. A large number of workers 
who were working under the Respondent Contractor also were 
members of the Petitioner Union, On 6-12-1976, Krishnan 
was issued a charge sheet and was directed to offer his 
explanation within 8 hours. I he charge against him was that 
he brought an old 'Manvetti' with an intention of replacing 
it with a new one and taking away stealthly the new one. 
The worker appeared before the Management and wanted time 
for submitting a. written explanation, but be was asked imme- 
diately to lilc a written explanation. As Krishnan did not know 
to write, the Management directed him to go to a Chettiar 
scribe, who was a good friend of the Management for assist- 
ance. The scribe wrote something. He affixed his thumb im- 
pression. He was dismissed after filing of his explanation 
even without enquiry. The Management acted arbitrarily with- 
out giving a reasonable opportunity to make his representation 
and conduct his defence. The Management did not give him 
a second show cause notice as per Model Standing Orders 
14(c) asking him to show cause against the punishment, viz. 
dismissal. The enquiry was done in a hurried manner violating 
the principles of natural justice. Such a charge was invented 
by the Management to victimise him as he happened to 
be the very active member of the Union. On coming to 
know the dismissal, the Union passed a resolution to take 
up the matter and fight for the right of the worker. The 
Union representatives wrote a letter to the Regional Labour 
Commissioner (Central) Madras and he had advised the 
Management to take him back. But the Management did not 
agree. In these circumstances, the reference had been made 
to this Tribunal to enquire into the matter and set aside the 
dismissal and to reinstate him in service with continuity ol 
service and other benefits. 

(4) A counter statement was filed by the Respondent- 
Management denying all the allegations against the Petitioner 
Union. The Management contended that the Union had no 
representative character to expose the cause of the worker 
Krishnan and as such the reference at the instance of the 
Pctitinoer Union is not maintainable. On merits the Peti- 
tioner Union has no case. The allegations that the enquiry 
was hurried through and there was violation of the principles 
of natural justice are not true. The Petitioner Union cannot 
seek remedy against the Management as he is only a small 
independent Contractor employing about less than 50 workers 
and the model standing order would not apply to him. There- 
for the question of non-compliancc with provision of standing 
order 14(c) of the Model Standing Order would not come 
into piay. Even assuming that 14(c) of the Model Standing 
Order was applicable, he never made representation to the 
Management that he should be given opportunity to defend 
his case and allow any representation to appear on his behalf 
to contest the matter. It is a case where the delinquent plead- 
ing guilty of charge and that being so he cannot be heard 
to say that there was no proper domestic enquiry. The worker 
was incharge of the stores on 9-11-1976, He admitted to 
have committed theft of new Manvetti by practising a fraud 
on one Store-keeper Palanisamy by replacing new Manvetti 
with old one. Witnesses were examined to substantiate the 
charge and that will clearly go to establish the guilt of 
worker Krishnan. He had voluntarily pleaded guilty and his 
plea was accepted and dismissed him from service. He cannot 
plead that punishment was harsh, for, the misconduct he 
committed was very grave in nature which entail the only 
punishment viz., dismissal. Therefore the claim of (he Peti- 
tioner is liable to be dismissed. 

(5) On the side of the Union Ex. W-l and on the side of 
the Management Exs. M-l to M-6 were marked. But the 
parties wanted to settle the matter out of court and time 
was granted. 

(6) When the matter came to this Tribunal finally for 
hearing, the counsel for the Union presented a loint Memo 
stating that the worker Krishnan was willing to receive 
Rs. 1,200 in full and final settlement of his claim. On refusal 
of the Settlement, I find it is fair, reasonable and equiatble and 
hence it is recorded. 

(7) An Award is passed in terms of the Settlement, The 
settlement will form part of the Award.

New Delhi, the 15th February, 1978 

S.O. 573.— Tn pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Dis 
putes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government here 
by publishes the following award of the Central Governmen 
Industrial Tribunal, Jaipur in the industrial dispute hetweer 
the employers m relation to the management of Punjat 
National Bunk and (heir workmen, which was received bi 
the Central Government on the 4-2-78. 

CENTRAL INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, JAIPEJR 
Case No. C.I.T .— S of 1975 

REFERENCE : 

Government of India, Ministry of Labour Notificatioi 
No. L. 12012/27/73/LRII1 dated 23-7-75, 


In the matter of an Industrial Dispute 
BETWEEN 

The Regional Manager, Punjab National Bank, Jaipur, 
Region. 

AND 

Shin N. K. Soni, Clerk-eum-Godown keeper, Punjab 
Nulionnl Bank Jodhpur represented by All India 
PNB Employees Association. Delhi. 

APPEARANCE : 

For the Union. — Shri C. L. Bhardwaj. 

For the Bank. — Shri P, V. Balkrishan. 

Date of Award. — 18-1-78. 

AWARD 

By its notification cited above, the Central Government 
has referred the following Industrial Dispute to this Tribu- 
nal for adjudication : — 

“Whether the appointment of Shri N. K. Soni, Clcrk-cum- 
Godown keeper as Probationer, with effect from the 18th 
July, 1968 by the Punjab National Bank is justified ? If not, 
to what relief Is the said workman entitled, keeping in view 
his services wilh the said Bank from the 2nd December, 
1966 ?” 

As the terms of reference reveal the dispute is between 
(he management of the Punjab National Bank and its work- 
man Shri N. K. Soni. 11 relates to the date of his absorp- 
tion in the bunk services. The workman’s cause was jointly 
sponsored and espoused by two unions (1) All India Punjab 
National Bunk Employees Association and (2) The Asso- 
ciation of Punjab National Bank Employees, Rajasthan State, 
Ajmer. 

Succinctly stated, the ease set up by the unions in their 
statement of claims is that the concerned workman Shri N. 
K. Soni was appointed permanently on 2-12-1966 against 
the additional permanent sanctioned vacancy of clcrk-cum- 
godown keeper at Jodhpur branch. He continued to work 
there in that capacity upto 17-7-1968. In order to con- 
vert his permanent appointment into temporary appoint- 
ment, the hank gave him artificial breaks of iwo or three 
days on each occasion from 25-12-66 to 17-7-68. The total 
number of such break days comes to 13 in all. These 
breaks were introduced by the bank to deny the workman, 
continuity of service and confirmation after six months from 
the original appointment, which was bound to come in 
view of the provisions of bipartite settlement. The manage- 
ment further appointed one Shri H. L. Kankaria, as a pro- 
bationer on 5-2-1967, against the permanent sanctioned 
vacancy, on which Shri N. K. Soni was already working 
from 5-2-67. Again (he management of the Bank appointed 
one Shri Khem Singh as Godown keeper on 10-3-69. Later 
on Shri Khen Singh was permanently absorbed on 18-11-72. 
He was allowed annual increment taking 10-3-69 as his 
anniversary date. But this practice was said a good-bye in 
t he case of Shri N. K. Soni. Tn terms of para 20.8 of the 
bipartite settlement dated 19-19-1966, Shri N. K. Soni 
should have been confirmed after he had completed six 
months service. Similarly, he should have been allowed 
annual increment from 2-12-1967, The union first took 
the matter before the Staff Controller of the Bank, New 
Delhi to redress the grievance. It led to an exchange of 
letters between the management and the unions. But nothing 
successful could come out. The matter was then taken to 
the Conciliation Officer, but there too no conciliation could 
take place due to the non-cooperative attitude of the man- 
agement. The reliefs claimed are : — 

(1) Shri N. K. Soni be taken as confirmed employee 
after six months from the date of his original 
appointment i.e. 2-6-1967, 

(2) Wages for the artificial breaks, and 

(3) Annaul increments from 2nd December, 1967 and 
the date of 2nd December of every succeeding 
year, be taken and treated as anniversary date for 
earning the annual increment, 

The claim of the unions was resisted by the Bank. It was 
pleaded by the Bank in its statement of defence that the 
provision of Bipartite Settlement or awards were mis-con- 
ptrued and mis-understood by the unions. There were no 



602 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 25, 1978/PHALGUNA 6, 1899 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


provisions in these settlements and awards by which the 
date of first temporary appointment can be taken to be the 
date of permanent appointment, It was admitted that Shri 
N. K. Soni was appointed as a temporary hand on 2-12-1966. 
in stop-gap arrangement and worked in that capacity npto 
23-7- 1 y 6K with breaks. It was denied that Shri Soni’s 
appointment was permanent in nature or was made against 
any permanent vacancy. It was also denied that the breaks 
oceuring in Shri Soni's services were artificially introduced 
to deny confirmation or continuity of service. It was fur- 
ther alleged that as per recruitment Rules of the Bank, one 
has to qualify himself in the test for permanent absorption 
in the Bank services. Shri Soni qualified himself in the re- 
cruitment test held on I 1-6-1968. He was accordingly ab- 
sorbed in the Bank Service as probationer clcrk-cum-godown 
keeper w.e.f. 26-7-68, at Jodhpur-Branch. Since then he 
was working there. It was categorically denied that any 
preferential or unequal consideration was given to Sarvashri 
H. L. Kanknria or Khcnt Singh. It was further denied that 
in view of the provisions of awards and settlements, Shri 
N. K, Soni was entitled to be confirmed on his completing 
six month service. Likewise, it was also denied that be was 
entitled to earn increments w.e.f. 2-12-1967 or on 2nd Decem- 
ber of the succeeding years. In the end, it was submitted that 
the workman is not entitled to any relief claimed by him. 

In view of the rival contentions of the parties, the follow- 
ing questions arise for deliberations and decision. 

(1) Whether the initial appointment of the workman Shri 
N, K. Soni made on 2-12-1966 was permanent in nature 7 

( 2 ) If not, should his temporary employment be taken 
into account to make him permanent in view of the terms 
of various awards and settlements 7 

(3) Relief to follow ? 

Before proceeding further, some non-controversial facts 
which have the direct bearing on the case may be men- 
tioned. The concerned workman Shri N. K. Soni was ini- 
tially appointed in the clerical cHdre, on 2-12-1966 and was 
posted at the Bank’s Jodhpur Branch. The bank holds a 
test to recruit employees in the permanent cadre. The can- 
didates who come out successful in such tests arc taken as 
probationers and are subsequently absorbed in the perma- 
nent cadre. Shri N. K. Soni appeared in such a test in 
1968 and came out successful. He was then taken as pro- 
bationer on 11-6-1968 in permanent cadre and was con- 
firmed in due course. 

I shall now proceed to record my findings on the con- 
troversial question seriatim : — 

Re — 1 : Nature of appointment — whether permanent 

or temporary 7 

It would be proper lo state a few words about the tem- 
porary employees. The cadre of temporary employees has 
been kept, retained, maintained and recognised in various 
awards and bipartite settlement between the banking com- 
panies and their workmen. The earliest award was passed 
in 1952, which is popularly known as SASTRI award. Para 
508 of it relates to the classification of employees. The em- 
ployees were put into four categories, one of which relates 
to temporary employees. The expression “temporary em- 
ployees” has been defined in the latter part of the same 
para. 

The second and so far known to me, the last award 
popularly known as DESAI award was passed in 1962, 
Para 21.20 of it speaks about the qualification of employees. 
It recognizes the category of temporary employers. The 
scope of the expression “temporary employees 1 ' was exten- 
ded by including also an employee “who is appointed in a 
temporary vacancy of a permanent workman”. 

In point of time, the last document governing the service 
conditions between the parties is the bipartite settlement 
which they struck in October, 1966. Both the previous 
awards alluded to above by me were reviewed by this settle- 
ment. Para 20.7 of this bipartite settlement recognised cate- 
gory of temporary employees. The scopo of the expression 
“temporary employees’’ was once again extended and wi- 
dened by including “a workman who is appointed in a tem- 
porary vacancy caused by the absence of a particular perma- 
nent workman'’. 


It is thus crystal clear that the category of temporary 
employees has been throughout kept alive, retained and re- 
cognised. This category has been widened and enlarged from 
time to time by redefining the expression “temporary em- 
ployees". The latest position is that a person ean be lempo- 
rarily appointed on a permanent post in the absence of a 
permanent workman. 

These various provisions discussed above make it abun- 
dantly clear that simply by the appointment of a person 
against a permanent post, even if such appointment has been 
temporarily for stop-gap arrangement, he cannot be taken 
to have been appointed on a permanent basis. 

Now' turning to the instant case in my hand the admitted 
position is that the workman Shri N, K. Son was appointed 
on 2-12-1966. On 8-7-77, the parties submitted a joint state- 
ment of service record of Shri N. K. Soni. It contains in two 
annexures. They show that Shri N, K. Soni was appointed 
temporarily from lime to time for fixed terms. His ser- 
vices stood terminated from time to time. The services of 
a permanent employee are never terminated in such a 
manner. These breaks are possible only in the case of an 
employee of temporary nature. The fact that breaks occurred 
Irom time to time in the employment of Shri N. K. Soni 
strongly suggests that his appointment was purely on tem- 
porary basis. 

There is one more aspect which clearly shows that his 
initial appointment was of temporary nature. In the statement 
of claims submitted by the unions, it has been pleaded that 
the benefit of the provisions contained in para 20.8 of the 
bipartite settlement 1966 be given to the workmen. The 
provisions of para 20,8 apply only in the case of a tempo- 
rary workman. They do not apply and in my opinion there 
is no scope to apply them to a case of permanent employee. 

It is true that the bank has not submitted the appointment 
letters Issued to (he workman from time to time in 1966 and 
1967. It is also true that Ihe service records of Shri N. K. 
Soni have also not been submitted by the Bank. But the non- 
submission of lhe.se records do not convert his temporary 
employment into that of a permanent nature. 

For reasons discussed above, I hold that the initial app- 
ointment of Shri N. K. Soni made on 2-12-66 and the sub- 
sequent appointments made from time to time except that 
which was made on 26-7-1968 due lo his coming out succ- 
essful in Ihe competilive test, were temporary in nature. 

Re — 2 : Should the Initial appointment of Shri N. K. 

Soni be made permanent 7 

It was vehemently contended on behalf of the unions that 
Shri N. K. Soni should be given the benefit contained in 
para 20.8 of the bipartite settlement, 1966. It was argued 
that Shri Soni was selected for permanent absorption 
in the competilive test held on 11-6-68, Before that 
he was a temporary employee in the bank. The provisions 
contained in 20.8 of the bipartite settlement aptly apply to 
Shri Soni’s case. It was also argued that Shri N. K. Soni 
was temporarily appointed against a permanent post, In 
reply, it was submitted on behalf of the Bank that the bene- 
fit contained in para 20.8 is available only to an employee 
who has been appointed against a permanent post. Shri 
N. K. Soni was not appointed against any permanent post. 

I have taken the respective contentions into my thoughtful 
considerations. 

It would be profitable to reproduce pnras 20.7 and 20.8 
of the bipartite settlement. 

(iii) TEMPORARY EMPLOYEES 

“20.7 — In supression of paragraph 21.20 and sub-clause 
(c) of paragraph 23.15 of the Dcsai Award. “Temporary 
Employee” will mean a workman who has been appointed for 
a limited period for work which is of an essentially tempo- 
rary nature or who is employed temporarily as an additional 
workman in connection with a fempornry increase in work 
of a permanent nature and includes a workman other than 
a permanent workman who is appointed in a temporary 
vacancy caused by the absence of a particular permanent 
workman. 

“20,8 — A temporary workman may also be appointed to 
fill a permanent vacancy provided that such temporary 
appointment shull not exceed a period of three months dur- 



[*rnr 3(ii)J 


603 


wrw Tt ws: tFntTf 25, tays/Wpi e, ieu 9 


ing which the bank shall make arrangements for filling up 
the vacancy permanently. If such a temporary workman is 
eventually selected for filling up the vacancy, the period of 
such temporary employment wall be taken into account as 
part of his probationary period." 


In the peculiar circumstances of the case I leave the parties 
to bear their own costs of this proceeding. 

The award be submitted to the Central Government for 
publication as required by law. 


Para 20.7 makes the provision that a workman can be 
appointed in a temporary capacity against a permanent post, 
t ile unions examined the concerned workman Shri N. K. Soni 
to establish that his appointment was made against a perma- 
nent post. Shrl Soni deposed that he was appointed by the 
flank on 2-12-1966 against an additional permanent sanctioned 
vacancy. He further deposed that no appointment letter was 
issued to him then and there after whenever he was re-em- 
ployed by (he Bank. Shri Soni was cross-examined at some 
length but his testimony on this point remained unshaken, and 
un-punctured. There arc no good grounds to put his testi- 
mony at a discount. 

In the rebuttal, the Bank adduced no evidence. Thus there 
is nothing in f he bank’s side to countenance the sworn testi- 
mony of the workman Shri N. K. Soni. 

There are several other grounds which speak volumes 
against the bank. The awards have made it incumbent on 
the bank to issue appointment letters in writing to the per- 
son appoinlcd in the bank service, irrespective of the fact 
whether the appointment Is of temporary or permanent 
nature. Para 495 of SASTRI award and para 23.20 of 
DESAI award contain these mandates. The bank has not 
issued any appointment letters in writing to Shri N. K. Soni, 
The bank has also not submitted the appointment letters be- 
fore me. It leads to the inference that if these appointment 
letters were produced in evidence, they would have gone and 
spoken against the bank. 


S. S. BYAS, Judge, Presiding Officer 
[F. No. L-12012/27 /73-LR-III(D,H.A)]

hereby publishes the following award of the Central Govern-

BEFORE THIRU K. SFI.VARATNAM, B.A., B.L„ 
PRESIDING OFFICER, 

INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, MADRAS. 
(Constituted by the Central Government) 

Saturday, the 7lh day of January, 1978 
Industrial Dispute No. 57 of 1976 

(In the matter of the dispute for adjudication under 
Section 10(l)(d) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
between the workmen and the Management of Indian 
Overseas Bank, Madras). 


Para 56 of SASTRI award makes a provision for the main- 
tenance of service books of the Bank employees. It provides 
that in the case of every employee whether he is temporary, 
or probationer or permanent, a service book must be main- 
tained. It is expected that the bank must have maintained the 
service book of Shri N. K. Soni. But this service book 
has not been submitted in evidence by the Bank for reasons 
best known to it. It again pushes me to draw an inference 
against the batik that the service book of Shri N. K. Soni 
was not produced in evidence before nte because in it his 
appointment was shown to have been made against a per- 
manent vacancy. 


Taking all these factors into consideration. I am of the 
opinion that Shri N. K. Son! was appointed temporarily on 
2-12-66 against the additional permanent sanctioned vacancy. 

I shall next turn to the provisions of para 20.8 of the 
bipartite settlement. As a general rule, the temporary em- 
ployment Is not counted and Is not taken into account as 
E nrt e Probationary period. The provisions contained In 
para 20.8 makes a departure front this general rule. This 
para Introduces a legal fiction that if a temporary workman 
is eventually selected for filling up the permanent vacancy, 
the period of temporary employment will be taken into 
account as part of the probationary period. Shri N. K. Soni 
was selected to fill up the permanent vacancy in a competitive 
est held on 11-6-68. Before that he was a temporary em- 
ployee. He was a temporary employee, since 2-12-1966 By 
virtue of the provisions contained in para 20.8 of the bi- 
partite settlement, Shri N. K. Soni Is entitled to contain that 
the period of his temporary employment since 2-12-1966 
period bC * akCn mt ° aCCmint DS pnrt of his Probationary 

T accordingly bold that the period of temporary employment 
of Shn N. K. Som commencing from 2-121966 shall be 
taken into account as part of his probafronery period. 

Re — 3 : Relief to follow ? 


_ cons ® ( ltience to my above findings on points No. 1 a 
2, 1 hold that the workman Shri N. K. Soni is entitled 
reap the benefit contained in para 20.8 of the bipartite s eft 
ment, 1966. His temporary service period from 2-12-1S 
shall be counted and taken into consideration as part of 
Probationary P eri ? d ' He will get ,,11 the benefits (lowing fre 
he provisions of para 20.8 of the aforesaid settlement 
make ray award accordingly.

BETWEEN 

The workmen represented by

REFERENCE : 


Order No. F. No. L-1201 1 /34/76-D. II. A, dated 
30-10-1976 of the Ministry of Labotir, Government 
of Tndia, New Delhi. 


This dispute coming on this day for hearing upon perusing 
the reference, claim and counter statements and all other 
material papers on record and upon hearing of Thira R. Jamal 
Mazocm for Thiruvalargal Aiyar and Doha, advocates for 
the workmen and of Thiru N.G.R. Prasad, advocate for the 
Management and the parties having filed a memorandum of 
Settlement and recording the same, this Tribunal made the 
following. 


AWARD 

Th |s f. reference by the Government of India under Sec- 
tion I0( I)(d) of tlie Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 for adjudi- 
cation of an Industrial Dispute between the Management of 
.Indian Overseas Bank and their workmen. 

2. The point of reference to this Tribunal ia as follows : 

Whether the demand of the All India Overseas Bank 
Employees Union, Madras for treatment of all 
Sweepers employed in all areas in Indian Overseas 
Hank, 151, Mount Road, Madras as part-time emp- 
loyees instead of temporary employees and to grant 
them all benefits as per paragraph 6(b) of the Settle- 
ment dated 17-12-1970 between the management oi 
the Bank and the All India Overseas Bank Emp 
loyees Union is justified 7 If so, to what benefits 
are the said workmen entitled and from what date 7 

tend I s h foitaS*: fflcd " C,aim S,atcment ’ wh erein *cy con- 


The Indian Overseas Bank is a nationalised Bank and has 
branches' all over the India. They employ Sweepers to 
sweep and clean the premises, furniture and fetch drinklna 
water on part time basis and their services have not been re- 
gularised for a long time and they are paid only a meagre 



604 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 25, 1978/PHALGUNA 6. 1899 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


salary of Rs. 20 per month, contrary to the Settlement on 
14-12-1966 arrived at under the Industrial Disputes Act. The 
Settlement inter alia dealt with scales of pay of full-time 
workmen as well os part-time workmen. The category of 
part-time workmen was sub-divided into subordinate staff and 
non-subordinate staff. Under the Settlement, the graduated 
incremental pay scale relating to the working hours of part- 
tinto workmen was laid down. The Management has not 
chosen to extend the scales of wages to the part-time Sweepers 
for the reasons best known to them. The Sweepers took up 
the matter before the Conciliation Officer and there was a 
conciliation proceedings and a Settlement was arrived at on 
24th May, 1973 under Section 12(3) of the Industrial Dis- 
putes Act. Even after the Settlement, the Management has 
not granted proportionate increment in accordance with para 
6(b) of the settlement dated 17-12-1970 and also they re- 
fused other benefits. The Management also declined to 
pay the incremental pay-scales on the ground that 
the sweepers are temporary. In these circumstances, the 
Sweepers employed by the Management are entitled to all 
benefits in accordance with the terms of the Settlement 
dated 17-12-1970 as applicable to part-time workmen read 
with the settlement dated 14-12-1966. 

(4) The Management filed a counter, wherein they con- 
tend as follows : The Sweepers were employed on part-time 
basis and their hours of work may not be more than a 
couple of hours and for the work they did they were origi- 
nally paid wages at Rs. 20 per month and they were not 
employed as part-time workmen and as such they were not 
entitled to proportionate wages and increments according to 
para 3.5(b) of the settlement dated 14-12-1966 and para 
6(b) of the subsequent settlement dated 17-12-1970 between 
the Claimnnt Union and the Bank. The Union raised a dis- 
pute in 1971 before the Regional Labour Commissioner 
(Central), Madras claiming salary and allowances payable 
to part-time workmen under the Settlement dated 17-12-1970, 
but their claim was resisted by the Management stating 
that they were employed only on a temporary and ad hoc 
basis under terms of the Settlement dated 12-7-1973 under 
Section 12(3) of the Industrial Disputes Act. Under the said 
settlement, it was agreed that the Sweepers would be paid 
wages only (not increments). The Bank was paying the 
Sweepers wages as per agreement dated 12-7-1973 and they 
had not contravened any settlement as alleged in the Claim 
Statement, As a result of the above settlement, a Sweeper 
originally getting Rs. 20 is now getting a minimum of Rs. 135 
per menstim, They were paid pro-ratu bonus also. In the 
face of the Settlement they can claim only wages us per the 
terms in para 6(h) of the Settlement dated 17-12-1970 and 
while the Settlement is in force, they could not raise a dis- 
pute without terminating the Settlement dated 12-7-1973. On 
this ground alone, the reference has to be rejected inlinine. 
Therefore, the present demand is not justified and is also not 
maintainable. 

(5) Today, when the matter came up for enquiry' both sides 
filed a Settlement, under which, the Management agreed to 
extend the benefit of wages applicable to part-time workmen 
to Sweepers and also other benefits applicable to the part- 
time workmen. The Management also agreed to extend the 
benefits set out in Chapter 15 of the Settlement dated 
14-12-1966 to Sweepers with effect from 1-1-1978, 

(6) On perusal of the terms of the Settlement, I find 
that the Settlement arrived at is fair and reasonable. Hence 
1 accept the Settlement and pass an Award in terms of the 
Settlement dated 5-1-1978, which will form part of the Award- 

Dated, this 7th day of January, 1978. 

K. SF.LVARATNAM, Presiding Officer

Whereas the settlement dated 17 1 2-1970 between the

among other things, wages and increments payable to part 
time workmen;

that the sweepers engaged by the Bank should bo treated as 
part lime workmen and given the benefit of the settlement 
dated 17-12-1970; 

And whereas this ended in a settlement dated 12-7-1973 
under Sec. 12(3) of the I.D. Act under which the Manage- 
ment agreed to give the sweepers employed in Area I and II 
the wages only as per paragraph 6(b) ol the settlement 
dated 17-12-1970; and the Union agreed that their demand 
relating to sweepers employed in Areas I and II were fully 
met by that Settlement; 

And whereas the Management has been paying the swee- 
pers wages as agreed to in the Settlement dated 12-7-1973 
and whereas the Union has now raised a dispute demanding 
that the sweepers should be paid also the increments and 
other benefits that arc payable to part time workmen under 
the Settlement dated 17-12-1970;

judication to the Central Government Industrial Tribunal at 
Madras and is pending as I.D. 57/1976; 

And whereas the Union has approached the Management 
and requested, them in the interest of maintaining goodwill 
and cordial relationship with the Management consider grant- 
ing the sweepers the other benefits ns per Para 6(b) of the 
Settlement dated 17-12-1970;

agreed to the following ; 

Terms of Settlement

with effect from 1-1-1974.

ed in Chapter 15 of the Settlement dated 14-12-1966 
to sweepers with effect from 1-1-1978. 

3. The Union agrees that the claim of (he workmen 

have been fully met by this Settlement and the 
parties pray for an award in the above terms.

MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 25th February, 1978 

S.O, 57J. — YVhercns for the development of the Export 
trade of India, certain proposals for subjecting Rosin to 
Quality, Control and Inspection prior to export, were published 
as required by sub-rule (2) of rule 11 of the Export (Quality 
Control and Inspection) Rules, 1964 in the Gazette of Tndia, 
Part-ll-Section-3-Sub-section (ii), dated the 19th February, 
1977, under the order of the government of Tndia in the 
Ministry of Commerce No. S.O. 643, dated the 19th Feb- 
ruary, 1 977; 

And whereas the objections and suggestions were invited 
till 4th April, 1977 from all persons likely to be affected 
thereby; 

And whereas the copies of the said Gazette were made 
available to the public on the 26th February, 1977; 

And whereas the objections and suggestions received from 
the public on the said draft have been considered by the 
Central Government; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of (he powers conferred by 
section 6 of the Export (Quality Control and Inspection) 
Act. 1963 (22 of 1963) the Central Government, after con- 
spiring the Export Inspection Council, being of opinion that 
it is necessary and expedient so to do for the development 
of export trade of India, hereby — 

(i) notifies that rosin shall be subject to inspection 
prior to export; 

(ii) recognises — 

(a) the specification for rosin ns set out in the An- 
nexure to this order, as standard specifications 
for rosin, 

(b) the specifications in the export contract, provided 
that such specifications do not fall bciow the 
requirements specified in the said Annexuie. 

(iii) Specifics the type of inspection in accordance with 
the /Export of Rosin (Inspection) Rules, 1978 as 
(he type of inspection which would be applied to 
such rosin, prior to its export. 

(iv) Prohibits the exn-orl in the course of international 
trade of such rosin, unless the same is accompanied by a 
certificate, issued by any of the agencies established under 
section 7 of the Exnort (Quality Control and Inspection) 
Act, 1963 (22 of 1963) to the effect that the rosin con- 
forms to the aforesaid standard specifications. 

3. Nothing in this order shall apply to the export bv land, 
sea or air of samples of rosin to the prospective buyers 
provided the F.O.B, value of the consignment does not 
exceed rupees one hundred and twentyflve, 

4. Tn this order “Rosin" means tire material by whatever 
name called, derived from Oleorcsin of pine which maV be 
in the form of transparent or slightly translucent brittle 
lumps with a glassy fracture.
